---README---
# OKR Viewer

A 3D OKR tree you can interrogate and edit by talking to it.

Load an org's objectives and key results as a navigable 3D graph, then ask questions in plain language — "which KRs don't ladder up to anything?", "make the marketing KRs measurable" — and watch Claude answer *by moving the visualization*: flying the camera to the nodes it's talking about, and rewriting them in place when you ask it to.

The pitch in one line: **the model's output is the interface, not a chat log next to one.**

---

## Demo script

This is the thing to build toward. Everything below serves these 90 seconds.

1. **Open on the full tree.** One company objective at the top, team objectives beneath it, KRs at the leaves. Rotate it. It's obviously a hierarchy and you can see all of it at once.
2. **Ask a question.** "Which key results don't clearly support a company objective?" → Claude answers in the panel *and* three nodes pulse red while the camera flies to them.
3. **Ask for a fix.** "Rewrite those so they're measurable and tie them to the right objective." → the nodes' text changes, edges re-link, the graph settles into a new shape.
4. **Undo it.** One click, tree snaps back. (This also saves you if the model does something dumb on stage.)

---

## Architecture

```
┌──────────────────────┐         ┌─────────────────────┐         ┌────────┐
│  web/  (Vite+Svelte) │  POST   │  service/ (Express) │  HTTPS  │ Claude │
│                      │ /api/   │                     │         │  API   │
│  owns the tree ──────┼─ chat ──┼─> owns the prompt    ├────────>│        │
│  renders the graph   │<────────┼── + tool schema      │<────────┤        │
│  applies actions     │ {reply, │   validates IDs      │         └────────┘
│  keeps undo stack    │  actions│                      │
└──────────────────────┘  highlight}────────────────────┘
```

**The service is stateless.** The browser owns the tree and sends it along with every message. No session store, no database, no drift between what the service thinks the tree is and what's on screen.

**The service owns the system prompt and the tool schema.** The frontend never constructs a prompt. This means prompt iteration — which is most of your tuning time — is a service-only change.

---

## The contract

One endpoint. One shape. Don't add a second one.

```
POST /api/chat
{
  "tree":           { "nodes": [...] },     // the whole thing, every time
  "message":        "make the marketing KRs measurable",
  "selectedNodeId": "kr-7" | null,          // what the user clicked, for context
  "history":        [{ "role": "user"|"assistant", "content": "..." }]
}

→ 200
{
  "reply":     "I tightened three KRs under Marketing…",
  "actions":   [{ "op": "edit", "id": "kr-7", "fields": { "label": "…" } }],
  "highlight": ["kr-7", "kr-8", "kr-9"]
}
```

`actions` is often empty — most messages are questions, not edits. `highlight` is almost never empty, and it's what makes the thing feel alive.

### Node shape

Keep it flat. A flat array with `parent` pointers is easier to mutate than nested children, and `3d-force-graph` wants flat nodes and links anyway.

```js
{
  id:          'kr-7',
  level:       'company' | 'objective' | 'kr',
  parent:      'obj-2' | null,
  label:       'Reduce time-to-first-value for new accounts',
  owner:       'Marketing',
  metric:      'median days from signup to first workflow run',
  target:      '14 → 5 by Q4',
  contributes: 0.8            // 0–1, how well this supports its parent
}
```

`contributes` is the field that earns the visualization. Edge thickness and color come from it, and low-scoring links are the "misalignment" the demo finds. Hand-author these values — it's a demo, and a hand-tuned tree demos better than a realistic one.

---

## The one-tool trick

Do **not** define `edit_node`, `add_node`, `relink_node`, and `highlight_nodes` as four separate tools. Real multi-tool use means Claude stops at the `tool_use` block, you send back a `tool_result`, and you make a second call to get the final text — two round trips, continuation logic, and a branch for "what if it called no tools at all."

Instead: **one tool, forced.**

```js
const RESPOND_TOOL = {
  name: 'respond',
  description: 'Reply to the user about their OKR tree. Always call this exactly once.',
  input_schema: {
    type: 'object',
    properties: {
      reply: {
        type: 'string',
        description: 'Your answer, shown in the chat panel. Conversational, 1-3 sentences.'
      },
      actions: {
        type: 'array',
        description: 'Edits to apply to the tree. Empty array if the user only asked a question.',
        items: {
          type: 'object',
          properties: {
            op:     { type: 'string', enum: ['edit', 'add', 'relink', 'delete'] },
            id:     { type: 'string' },
            fields: { type: 'object' }
          },
          required: ['op', 'id']
        }
      },
      highlight: {
        type: 'array',
        description: 'IDs of nodes your reply refers to. The camera flies to these. Use generously.',

Tool reply2026-09-11T20:25:55.736Z
`vite.config.js` proxies `/api` → `:8787`, so there's no CORS to fight in dev:

```js
server: { proxy: { '/api': 'http://localhost:8787' } }
```

The key lives only in `service/.env` and never reaches the browser. `.env` is gitignored — check that before your first commit, not after.

Model ID goes in one place (`service/prompt.js`). Check <https://docs.claude.com/en/docs/about-claude/models> for the current Sonnet-class ID rather than trusting a hardcoded string from a template.

---

## Layout

```
okr-viewer/
├── package.json                npm workspace root; one lockfile for all three
├── schema/                     @okr-viewer/schema — the shape, owned in one place
│   ├── src/*.schema.json       draft-07 schemas: node, tree, company, history,
│   │                           action, chat request/response, document envelope
│   ├── scripts/build.mjs       → dist/types.d.ts, dist/validators.js, tool schema
│   ├── index.js                validate*(), checkTreeSemantics(), create/parseDocument
│   ├── version.js              SCHEMA_VERSION, the document format version
│   ├── migrations/             one function per major step (empty at v1)
│   └── test/                   fixtures + round-trip tests
├── web/
│   ├── src/
│   │   ├── App.svelte          layout, owns tree state + undo stack, export/import
│   │   ├── Graph.svelte        3d-force-graph, bind:this + onMount
│   │   ├── Chat.svelte         message list, input, loading state
│   │   ├── Detail.svelte       selected-node sidebar
│   │   ├── lib/tree.js         the hand-written OKR tree
│   │   └── lib/apply.js        applyAction(tree, action) → new tree
│   └── vite.config.js
└── service/
    ├── index.js                POST /api/chat
    ├── prompt.js               system prompt + RESPOND_TOOL
    └── validate.js             drop actions/highlights with unknown IDs
```

`schema/dist/` is generated and gitignored. Edit `schema/src/`, run
`npm run build:schema`, and both consumers pick the change up.

---

## Build order

Each step is shippable on its own. If the clock runs out, you stop at a checkpoint with something that demos — not a half-wired step 4.

| # | Step | Est. | If you stop here |
|---|------|------|------------------|
| 1 | Hand-write `tree.js`, render with `3d-force-graph` (`dagMode: 'td'`), click → camera + detail panel | 50m | A working 3D OKR browser |
| 2 | Chat panel → `/api/chat`, whole tree in the system prompt, Claude answers questions | 30m | "Ask your OKRs anything" — already demoable |
| 3 | Honor `highlight[]`: camera fly-to + pulse | 20m | **The wow.** Answers move the view |
| 4 | Honor `actions[]`: apply, re-render, push undo snapshot | 40m | The full pitch |

**Protect steps 3 and 4 by keeping step 1 dumb.** Hand-author the JSON. No importer, no file upload, no editor UI. Twenty-five nodes is plenty and fits in a prompt with room to spare.

Stretch, only if you're ahead: highlight-by-hover on chat text, a `contributes` threshold slider, export the edited tree as JSON.

---

## Things that will bite you

**Claude will invent node IDs.** Not often, but it will, and an unknown ID either throws in your renderer or silently no-ops in the middle of a demo. You have the tree right there in the request — filter `actions` and `highlight` against it server-side and drop what doesn't resolve. Ten minutes of work on the single most likely live failure.

**Keep the graph instance out of Svelte's reactive system.** `3d-force-graph` mutates its own internals and does its own render loop; it fights `$state` if you let it. Create it once in `onMount` into a plain `let graph`, and push updates imperatively:

```js
$effect(() => { graph?.graphData(toGraphData(tree)); });
```

**Don't stream.** ~2k tokens in, a few hundred out — that's 2–4 seconds. A spinner is fine. Streaming tool-use blocks is fiddly enough to eat a meaningful slice of a two-hour budget for no demo value.

**Snapshot before you mutate.** `undoStack.push(structuredClone(tree))` before applying actions. Five lines, one button, and it turns a bad model response during a live demo from a dead end into "watch, I'll just back that out."

**Re-heating the layout on every edit is jarring.** After applying actions, prefer updating node objects in place over replacing the whole dataset, or the force simulation reshuffles the entire tree and the viewer loses their place.

---

## Scope boundary

Explicitly out for v1, listed here so it stays out:

- Auth, multi-user, persistence — the tree lives in memory and resets on reload
- Importing real OKRs from anywhere
- Streaming responses
- Mobile layout
- Tests

42	    41	        headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' },
43	    42	        body: JSON.stringify({ tree, message, selectedNodeId: selectedId, history })
44	    43	      });
45	    44	      if (!res.ok) {
46	    45	        const body = await res.json().catch(() => ({}));
47	    46	        const why = body.error ?? `${res.status} ${res.statusText}`;
48	    47	        throw new Error(body.details?.length ? `${why} (${body.details.slice(0, 3).join('; ')})` : why);
49	    48	      }
50	    49	      // The service validates what it sends, but this is the boundary the tree
51	    50	      // crosses before it reaches the renderer — so check it here too, and
52	    51	      // refuse to apply anything from a response we do not recognise.
53	    52	      const checked = validateChatResponse(await res.json());
54	    53	      if (!checked.ok) {
55	    54	        throw new Error(`the service sent a response this app can't read (${checked.errors.slice(0, 3).join('; ')})`);
56	    55	      }
57	    56	      const { reply, actions, highlight: ids } = checked.value;
58	    57	      if (actions.length) {
59	    58	        undoStack = [...undoStack, structuredClone(tree)]; // snapshot before we mutate
60	    59	        tree = applyActions(tree, actions);
61	    60	      }
62	    61	      highlight = ids;
63	    62	      messages.push({ role: 'assistant', content: reply, highlight: ids, actions: actions.length });
64	    63	    } catch (err) {
65	    64	      messages.push({ role: 'assistant', content: `Something went wrong: ${err.message}`, error: true });
66	    65	    } finally {
67	    66	      loading = false;
68	    67	    }
69	    68	  }
70	    69	
71	    70	  function undo() {
72	    71	    if (!undoStack.length) return;
73	    72	    tree = undoStack[undoStack.length - 1];
74	    73	    undoStack = undoStack.slice(0, -1);
75	    74	    highlight = [];
76	    75	    messages.push({ role: 'assistant', content: 'Reverted the last change.', note: true });
77	    76	  }
78	    77	
79	    78	  function reset() {
80	    79	    undoStack = [...undoStack, structuredClone(tree)];
81	    80	    tree = structuredClone(initialTree);
82	    81	    highlight = [];
83	    82	    selectedId = null;
84	    83	  }
85	    84	
86	    85	  // ── export / import ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
87	    86	  //
88	    87	  // The file is an OkrDocument: the tree plus a schemaVersion the reader checks
89	    88	  // before it trusts anything inside. Same package on both sides, so a file
90	    89	  // written here is a file the service would accept.
91	    90	
92	    91	  let fileInput;
93	    92	
94	    93	  function exportTree() {
95	    94	    const doc = createDocument({ tree, meta: { title: 'OKR Viewer export' } });
96	    95	    const url = URL.createObjectURL(new Blob([JSON.stringify(doc, null, 2)], { type: 'application/json' }));
97	    96	    const a = document.createElement('a');
98	    97	    a.href = url;
99	    98	    a.download = `okr-${new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)}.json`;
100	    99	    a.click();
101	   100	    URL.revokeObjectURL(url);
102	   101	    messages.push({ role: 'assistant', content: `Exported ${tree.nodes.length} nodes to ${a.download}.`, note: true });
103	   102	  }
104	   103	
105	   104	  async function importTree(e) {
106	   105	    const file = e.target.files?.[0];
107	   106	    e.target.value = ''; // so re-picking the same file fires change again
108	   107	    if (!file) return;
109	   108	
110	   109	    const out = parseDocument(await file.text());
111	   110	    if (!out.ok) {
112	   111	      messages.push({
113	   112	        role: 'assistant',
114	   113	        content: `Couldn't import ${file.name}: ${out.errors.slice(0, 3).join('; ')}`,
115	   114	        error: true
116	   115	      });
117	   116	      return;
118	   117	    }
119	   118	
120	   119	    undoStack = [...undoStack, structuredClone(tree)]; // same snapshot-then-replace as reset()
121	   120	    tree = out.document.tree;
122	   121	    highlight = [];
123	   122	    selectedId = null;
124	   123	    const warned = out.warnings.length ? ` ${out.warnings.length} warning${out.warnings.length === 1 ? '' : 's'}: ${out.warnings.slice(0, 3).join('; ')}` : '';
125	   124	    messages.push({
126	   125	      role: 'assistant',
127	   126	      content: `Imported ${tree.nodes.length} nodes from ${file.name}. Undo to go back.${warned}`,
128	   127	      note: true
129	   128	    });
130	   129	  }
131	   130	
132	   131	  function select(id) {
133	   132	    selectedId = id;
134	   133	  }
135	   134	
136	   135	  function recall(ids) {
137	   136	    highlight = [...ids]; // new array so the graph effect re-runs
138	   137	  }
139	   138	
140	   139	  function onKey(e) {
141	   140	    const tag = e.target?.tagName;
142	   141	    if (tag === 'INPUT' || tag === 'TEXTAREA') return;
143	   142	    if ((e.metaKey || e.ctrlKey) && e.key === 'z') {
144	   143	      e.preventDefault();
145	   144	      undo();
146	   145	    }
147	   146	  }
148	   147	</script>
149	   148	
150	   149	<svelte:window onkeydown={onKey} />
151	   150	
152	   151	<div class="app">
153	   152	  <header class="topbar">
154	   153	    <div class="brand"><span class="dot"></span> OKR Viewer</div>
155	   154	    <div class="stats">
156	   155	      {tree.nodes.length} nodes · <span class:warn={weakLinks}>{weakLinks} weak link{weakLinks === 1 ? '' : 's'}</span>
157	   156	    </div>
158	   157	    <div class="buttons">
159	   158	      <button onclick={undo} disabled={!undoStack.length} title="⌘Z">
160	   159	        ↶ Undo{undoStack.length ? ` (${undoStack.length})` : ''}
161	   160	      </button>
162	   161	      <button onclick={exportTree} class="ghost" title="Download the tree as a versioned JSON document">Export</button>
163	   162	      <button onclick={() => fileInput.click()} class="ghost" title="Replace the tree from a JSON document">Import</button>
164	   163	      <button onclick={reset} class="ghost">Reset</button>
165	   164	      <input bind:this={fileInput} onchange={importTree} type="file" accept="application/json,.json" hidden />
166	   165	    </div>
167	   166	  </header>
168	   167	
169	   168	  <main>
170	   169	    <section class="stage">
171	   170	      <Graph {tree} {selectedId} {highlight} onSelect={select} />
172	   171	      {#if selected}
173	   172	        <Detail node={selected} {tree} onSelect={select} onClose={() => (selectedId = null)} />
174	   173	      {/if}
175	   174	      <div class="legend">
176	   175	        <span><i class="sw company"></i> Company</span>
177	   176	        <span><i class="sw objective"></i> Objective</span>
178	   177	        <span><i class="sw kr"></i> Key result</span>
179	   178	        <span class="edge"><i class="sw line"></i> edge = how well it supports its parent</span>
180	   179	      </div>
181	   180	    </section>
182	   181	    <Chat {messages} {loading} onSend={send} onRecall={recall} />
183	   182	  </main>
184	   183	</div>
185	===== web/src/Graph.svelte =====
186	     1	<script>
187	     2	  import { onMount } from 'svelte';
188	     3	  import ForceGraph3D from '3d-force-graph';
189	     4	  import * as THREE from 'three';
190	     5	
191	     6	  let { tree, selectedId = null, highlight = [], onSelect } = $props();
192	     7	
193	     8	  let el;
194	     9	  // The graph instance lives outside Svelte's reactive system on purpose:
195	    10	  // 3d-force-graph mutates its own data and runs its own render loop.
196	    11	  let graph;
197	    12	  const meshes = new Map(); // node id → THREE.Mesh, for the pulse loop
198	    13	  let highlightSet = new Set();
199	    14	  let raf;
200	    15	
201	    16	  const LEVEL = {
202	    17	    company: { color: '#f5c542', size: 10 },
203	    18	    objective: { color: '#4f9cff', size: 6.5 },
204	    19	    kr: { color: '#5ad38a', size: 3.6 }
205	    20	  };
206	    21	  const WHITE = new THREE.Color('#ffffff');
207	    22	  const RED = new THREE.Color('#ff3b30');
208	    23	
209	    24	  // contributes 0 → red, 1 → green
210	    25	  function linkColor(c) {
211	    26	    const t = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, c ?? 0.5));
212	    27	    return `hsl(${Math.round(120 * t)}, 75%, ${52 - 10 * t}%)`;
213	    28	  }
214	    29	
215	    30	  const esc = (s) => String(s ?? '').replace(/[&<>"]/g, (ch) => ({ '&': '&amp;', '<': '&lt;', '>': '&gt;', '"': '&quot;' })[ch]);
216	    31	
217	    32	  function makeNodeObject(node) {
218	    33	    const cfg = LEVEL[node.level] ?? LEVEL.kr;
219	    34	    const mesh = new THREE.Mesh(
220	    35	      new THREE.SphereGeometry(cfg.size, 24, 24),
221	    36	      new THREE.MeshLambertMaterial({ color: cfg.color, transparent: true, opacity: 0.95 })
222	    37	    );
223	    38	    mesh.userData.baseColor = new THREE.Color(cfg.color);
224	    39	    meshes.set(node.id, mesh);
225	    40	    return mesh;
226	    41	  }
227	    42	
228	    43	  // Reuse existing node objects (they carry x/y/z) so an edit nudges the
229	    44	  // layout instead of re-heating the whole simulation.
230	    45	  function toGraphData(t, prev) {
231	    46	    const prevById = new Map((prev?.nodes ?? []).map((n) => [n.id, n]));
232	    47	    const nodes = t.nodes.map((n) => {
233	    48	      const existing = prevById.get(n.id);
234	    49	      return existing ? Object.assign(existing, n) : { ...n };
235	    50	    });
236	    51	    const ids = new Set(nodes.map((n) => n.id));
237	    52	    const links = t.nodes
238	    53	      .filter((n) => n.parent && ids.has(n.parent))
239	    54	      .map((n) => ({ source: n.parent, target: n.id, contributes: n.contributes ?? 0.5 }));
240	    55	    return { nodes, links };
241	    56	  }
242	    57	
243	    58	  export function flyTo(ids, ms = 1200) {
244	    59	    if (!graph || !ids?.length) return;
245	    60	    const want = new Set(ids);
246	    61	    const nodes = graph.graphData().nodes.filter((n) => want.has(n.id) && Number.isFinite(n.x));
247	    62	    if (!nodes.length) return;
248	    63	    const c = { x: 0, y: 0, z: 0 };
249	    64	    for (const n of nodes) { c.x += n.x; c.y += n.y; c.z += n.z; }
250	    65	    c.x /= nodes.length; c.y /= nodes.length; c.z /= nodes.length;
251	    66	    let radius = 0;
252	    67	    for (const n of nodes) radius = Math.max(radius, Math.hypot(n.x - c.x, n.y - c.y, n.z - c.z));
253	    68	    const dist = Math.max(140, radius * 2.2 + 120);
254	    69	    // Approach along the camera's current bearing so the move feels continuous.
255	    70	    const cam = graph.cameraPosition();
256	    71	    const dir = new THREE.Vector3(cam.x - c.x, cam.y - c.y, cam.z - c.z);
257	    72	    if (dir.length() < 1) dir.set(0, 0.3, 1);
258	    73	    dir.normalize();
259	    74	    graph.cameraPosition({ x: c.x + dir.x * dist, y: c.y + dir.y * dist, z: c.z + dir.z * dist }, c, ms);
260	    75	  }
261	    76	
262	    77	  function animate() {
263	    78	    const pulse = 0.5 + 0.5 * Math.sin(performance.now() / 180);
264	    79	    for (const [id, mesh] of meshes) {
265	    80	      const hi = highlightSet.has(id);
266	    81	      const sel = id === selectedId;
267	    82	      const target = hi ? 1.3 + 0.35 * pulse : sel ? 1.35 : 1;
268	    83	      mesh.scale.setScalar(mesh.scale.x + (target - mesh.scale.x) * 0.2);
269	    84	      const mat = mesh.material;
270	    85	      if (hi) {
271	    86	        mat.color.copy(RED).lerp(WHITE, 0.25 * pulse);
272	    87	        mat.emissive.copy(RED);
273	    88	        mat.emissiveIntensity = 0.3 + 0.7 * pulse;
274	    89	      } else if (sel) {
275	    90	        mat.color.copy(mesh.userData.baseColor).lerp(WHITE, 0.5);
276	    91	        mat.emissive.copy(mesh.userData.baseColor);
277	    92	        mat.emissiveIntensity = 0.5;
278	    93	      } else {
279	    94	        mat.color.copy(mesh.userData.baseColor);
280	    95	        mat.emissiveIntensity = 0;
281	    96	      }
282	    97	    }
283	    98	    raf = requestAnimationFrame(animate);
284	    99	  }
285	   100	
286	   101	  onMount(() => {
287	   102	    graph = ForceGraph3D()(el)
288	   103	      .backgroundColor('#0b0f17')
289	   104	      .dagMode('td')
290	   105	      .dagLevelDistance(90)
291	   106	      .onDagError((loopIds) => console.warn('DAG cycle, falling back to free layout', loopIds))
292	   107	      .nodeId('id')
293	   108	      .nodeLabel((n) => `<div class="tip"><b>${esc(n.label)}</b><span>${esc(n.owner)}${n.target ? ' · ' + esc(n.target) : ''}</span></div>`)
294	   109	      .nodeThreeObject(makeNodeObject)
295	   110	      .linkWidth((l) => 0.5 + 2.8 * (l.contributes ?? 0.5))
296	   111	      .linkColor((l) => linkColor(l.contributes))
297	   112	      .linkOpacity(0.9)
298	   113	      .linkDirectionalParticles((l) => ((l.contributes ?? 0.5) >= 0.4 ? 2 : 0))
299	   114	      .linkDirectionalParticleWidth(1.4)
300	   115	      .linkDirectionalParticleSpeed(0.005)
301	   116	      .d3VelocityDecay(0.3)
302	   117	      .warmupTicks(80)
303	   118	      .onNodeClick((n) => { onSelect?.(n.id); flyTo([n.id], 800); })
304	   119	      .onBackgroundClick(() => onSelect?.(null));
305	   120	    graph.d3Force('charge').strength(-160);
306	   121	
307	   122	    graph.graphData(toGraphData(tree, null));
308	   123	    const ro = new ResizeObserver(() => graph.width(el.clientWidth).height(el.clientHeight));
309	   124	    ro.observe(el);
310	   125	    setTimeout(() => graph.zoomToFit(900, 60), 700);
311	   126	    animate();
312	   127	
313	   128	    return () => {
314	   129	      cancelAnimationFrame(raf);
315	   130	      ro.disconnect();
316	   131	      graph._destructor?.();
317	   132	    };
318	   133	  });
319	   134	
320	   135	  $effect(() => {
321	   136	    const t = tree;
322	   137	    if (!graph) return;
323	   138	    graph.graphData(toGraphData(t, graph.graphData()));
324	   139	    const alive = new Set(t.nodes.map((n) => n.id));
325	   140	    for (const id of [...meshes.keys()]) if (!alive.has(id)) meshes.delete(id);
326	   141	  });
327	   142	
328	   143	  $effect(() => {
329	   144	    highlightSet = new Set(highlight);
330	   145	    if (highlight.length) flyTo(highlight);
331	   146	  });
332	   147	</script>
333	   148	
334	   149	<div class="graph" bind:this={el}></div>
335	===== web/src/Chat.svelte =====
336	     1	<script>
337	     2	  let { messages = [], loading = false, onSend, onRecall } = $props();
338	     3	
339	     4	  let text = $state('');
340	     5	  let listEl;
341	     6	
342	     7	  const suggestions = [
343	     8	    'Which key results don’t clearly support a company objective?',
344	     9	    'Rewrite those so they’re measurable and tie them to the right objective.',
345	    10	    'Which team is carrying the most risk?'
346	    11	  ];
347	    12	
348	    13	  $effect(() => {
349	    14	    void messages.length;
350	    15	    void loading;
351	    16	    requestAnimationFrame(() => listEl?.scrollTo({ top: listEl.scrollHeight, behavior: 'smooth' }));
352	    17	  });
353	    18	
354	    19	  function submit(e) {
355	    20	    e?.preventDefault();
356	    21	    const m = text.trim();
357	    22	    if (!m || loading) return;
358	    23	    text = '';
359	    24	    onSend(m);
360	    25	  }
361	    26	
362	    27	  function onKey(e) {
363	    28	    if (e.key === 'Enter' && !e.shiftKey) submit(e);
364	    29	  }
365	    30	</script>
366	    31	
367	    32	<aside class="chat">

371	    36	        <p>Ask the tree anything, or tell it what to change.</p>
372	    37	        {#each suggestions as s}

374	    39	        {/each}
375	    40	      </div>
376	    41	    {/if}
377	    42	    {#each messages as m}

379	    44	        <div class="bubble">{m.content}</div>
380	    45	        {#if m.role === 'assistant' && (m.highlight?.length || m.actions)}
381	    46	          <div class="meta">

383	    48	              <span class="pill edit">{m.actions} edit{m.actions === 1 ? '' : 's'}</span>
384	    49	            {/if}
385	    50	            {#if m.highlight?.length}
386	    51	              <button class="pill" onclick={() => onRecall(m.highlight)}>
387	    52	                show {m.highlight.length} node{m.highlight.length === 1 ? '' : 's'}
388	    53	              </button>
389	    54	            {/if}
390	    55	          </div>
391	    56	        {/if}
392	    57	      </div>
393	    58	    {/each}
394	    59	    {#if loading}
395	    60	      <div class="msg assistant"><div class="bubble thinking"><span></span><span></span><span></span></div></div>
396	    61	    {/if}

409	===== web/src/Detail.svelte =====
410	     1	<script>
411	     2	  let { node, tree, onSelect, onClose } = $props();
412	     3	
413	     4	  const LEVEL_NAME = { company: 'Company objective', objective: 'Team objective', kr: 'Key result' };
414	     5	  const parent = $derived(node.parent ? tree.nodes.find((n) => n.id === node.parent) ?? null : null);
415	     6	  const children = $derived(tree.nodes.filter((n) => n.parent === node.id));
416	     7	
417	     8	  const pct = (c) => Math.round((c ?? 0) * 100);
418	     9	  const tone = (c) => ((c ?? 0) < 0.4 ? 'bad' : (c ?? 0) < 0.7 ? 'meh' : 'good');
419	    10	</script>

440	    31	        <span class="pct {tone(node.contributes)}">{pct(node.contributes)}%</span>
441	    32	      </dd>

449	    40	          <button class="link" onclick={() => onSelect(c.id)}>{c.label}</button>

457	===== web/src/lib/apply.js =====
458	     1	// applyAction(tree, action) → new tree.
459	     2	//
460	     3	// Pure: never mutates the input. Untouched node objects are reused by
461	     4	// reference so the graph can diff by identity and keep its layout warm.
462	     5	//
463	     6	// The JSDoc types come from @okr-viewer/schema's generated declarations, so an
464	     7	// editor checks this file against the same schema the service validates with,
465	     8	// without either package adopting TypeScript.
466	     9	
467	    10	/**
468	    11	 * @typedef {import('@okr-viewer/schema').OkrTree} OkrTree
469	    12	 * @typedef {import('@okr-viewer/schema').OkrNode} OkrNode
470	    13	 * @typedef {import('@okr-viewer/schema').Action} Action
471	    14	 */
472	    15	
473	    16	const EDITABLE = ['label', 'owner', 'metric', 'target', 'contributes', 'parent'];
474	    17	
475	    18	const clamp01 = (n) => (Number.isFinite(n) ? Math.min(1, Math.max(0, n)) : 0.5);
476	    19	
477	    20	function pick(fields = {}, extra = []) {
478	    21	  const out = {};
479	    22	  for (const k of [...EDITABLE, ...extra]) if (k in fields) out[k] = fields[k];
480	    23	  if ('contributes' in out) out.contributes = clamp01(Number(out.contributes));
481	    24	  return out;
482	    25	}
483	    26	
484	    27	/**
485	    28	 * Every node below `id`, transitively.
486	    29	 * @param {OkrTree} tree
487	    30	 * @param {string} id
488	    31	 * @returns {Set<string>}
489	    32	 */
490	    33	export function descendants(tree, id) {
491	    34	  const out = new Set();
492	    35	  let frontier = [id];
493	    36	  while (frontier.length) {
494	    37	    const next = tree.nodes.filter((n) => frontier.includes(n.parent)).map((n) => n.id);
495	    38	    frontier = next.filter((x) => !out.has(x));
496	    39	    frontier.forEach((x) => out.add(x));
497	    40	  }
498	    41	  return out;
499	    42	}
500	    43	
501	    44	function wouldCycle(tree, id, newParent) {
502	    45	  if (!newParent) return false;
503	    46	  if (newParent === id) return true;
504	    47	  return descendants(tree, id).has(newParent);
505	    48	}
506	    49	
507	    50	/**
508	    51	 * Apply one action, returning a new tree. Anything that does not fit the tree
509	    52	 * (an unknown id, a parent that would make a cycle) is ignored rather than
510	    53	 * thrown — the service has already filtered the model's output.
511	    54	 * @param {OkrTree} tree
512	    55	 * @param {Action} action
513	    56	 * @returns {OkrTree}
514	    57	 */
515	    58	export function applyAction(tree, action) {
516	    59	  if (!action || typeof action.id !== 'string') return tree;
517	    60	  const { op, id } = action;
518	    61	  const exists = tree.nodes.some((n) => n.id === id);
519	    62	
520	    63	  switch (op) {
521	    64	    case 'edit':
522	    65	    case 'relink': {
523	    66	      if (!exists) return tree;
524	    67	      const fields = pick(action.fields);
525	    68	      if ('parent' in fields && wouldCycle(tree, id, fields.parent)) delete fields.parent;
526	    69	      if (!Object.keys(fields).length) return tree;
527	    70	      return { nodes: tree.nodes.map((n) => (n.id === id ? { ...n, ...fields } : n)) };
528	    71	    }
529	    72	
530	    73	    case 'add': {
531	    74	      if (exists) return applyAction(tree, { ...action, op: 'edit' });
532	    75	      const node = {
533	    76	        id,
534	    77	        level: 'kr',
535	    78	        parent: null,
536	    79	        label: '(untitled)',
537	    80	        owner: '',
538	    81	        metric: '',
539	    82	        target: '',
540	    83	        contributes: 0.5,
541	    84	        ...pick(action.fields, ['level'])
542	    85	      };
543	    86	      return { nodes: [...tree.nodes, node] };
544	    87	    }
545	    88	
546	    89	    case 'delete': {
547	    90	      if (!exists) return tree;
548	    91	      const gone = descendants(tree, id);
549	    92	      gone.add(id);
550	    93	      return { nodes: tree.nodes.filter((n) => !gone.has(n.id)) };
551	    94	    }
552	    95	
553	    96	    default:
554	    97	      return tree;
555	    98	  }
556	    99	}
557	   100	
558	   101	/**
559	   102	 * @param {OkrTree} tree
560	   103	 * @param {Action[]} actions
561	   104	 * @returns {OkrTree}
562	   105	 */
563	   106	export function applyActions(tree, actions = []) {
564	   107	  return actions.reduce(applyAction, tree);
565	   108	}

===== service/index.js =====
     1	import 'dotenv/config';
     2	import express from 'express';
     3	import Anthropic from '@anthropic-ai/sdk';
     4	import { MODEL, RESPOND_TOOL, systemPrompt } from './prompt.js';
     5	import { sanitizeHistory, mergeTurns, validateResponse } from './validate.js';
     6	import { checkTreeSemantics, validateChatRequest } from '@okr-viewer/schema';
     7	
     8	const PORT = Number(process.env.PORT) || 8787;
     9	const app = express();
    10	app.use(express.json({ limit: '2mb' }));
    11	
    12	// Created lazily so a missing key surfaces as a clear 500, not a crash at boot.
    13	let client;
    14	const getClient = () => (client ??= new Anthropic());
    15	
    16	app.get('/api/health', (_req, res) => res.json({ ok: true, model: MODEL }));
    17	
    18	app.post('/api/chat', async (req, res) => {
    19	  // One structural check for the whole body, straight from the shared schema.
    20	  const request = validateChatRequest(req.body);
    21	  if (!request.ok) {
    22	    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'The request does not match the chat schema.', details: request.errors });
    23	  }
    24	  const { tree, message, selectedNodeId = null, history = [] } = request.value;
    25	
    26	  // Shape is not enough: a tree can be well-formed and still be unusable.
    27	  // Warnings (an off-ladder link, say) are normal mid-edit, so they only log.
    28	  const semantics = checkTreeSemantics(tree);
    29	  if (semantics.errors.length) {
    30	    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'The tree is structurally broken.', details: semantics.errors });
    31	  }
    32	  if (semantics.warnings.length) console.warn('[chat] tree warnings:', JSON.stringify(semantics.warnings));
    33	
    34	  const selected = typeof selectedNodeId === 'string' && tree.nodes.some((n) => n.id === selectedNodeId) ? selectedNodeId : null;
    35	  const messages = mergeTurns([...sanitizeHistory(history), { role: 'user', content: message.trim() }]);
    36	
    37	  const started = Date.now();
    38	  try {
    39	    const response = await getClient().messages.create({
    40	      model: MODEL,
    41	      max_tokens: 4096,
    42	      system: systemPrompt(tree, selected),
    43	      tools: [RESPOND_TOOL],
    44	      tool_choice: { type: 'tool', name: 'respond' },
    45	      messages
    46	    });
    47	
    48	    const call = response.content.find((b) => b.type === 'tool_use' && b.name === 'respond');
    49	    if (!call) {
    50	      const text = response.content.filter((b) => b.type === 'text').map((b) => b.text).join('\n').trim();
    51	      const why = response.stop_reason === 'refusal' ? 'declined to answer' : `stopped early (${response.stop_reason})`;
    52	      console.warn(`[chat] no respond call: ${why}`);
    53	      return res.json({ reply: text || `The model ${why}. Try rephrasing.`, actions: [], highlight: [] });
    54	    }
    55	
    56	    const out = validateResponse(tree, call.input);
    57	    if (out.dropped.length) console.warn('[chat] dropped from model output:', JSON.stringify(out.dropped));
    58	    console.log(
    59	      `[chat] ${Date.now() - started}ms · ${response.usage.input_tokens}in/${response.usage.output_tokens}out · ${out.actions.length} actions · ${out.highlight.length} highlights`
    60	    );
    61	    res.json({ reply: out.reply, actions: out.actions, highlight: out.highlight });
    62	  } catch (err) {
    63	    // The SDK throws a plain Error (not AuthenticationError) when it finds no
    64	    // credentials at all, so match that case explicitly.
    65	    if (err instanceof Anthropic.AuthenticationError || /authentication method/i.test(err?.message ?? '')) {
    66	      console.error('[chat] auth failed — is ANTHROPIC_API_KEY set in service/.env?');
    67	      return res.status(500).json({ error: 'The service has no valid ANTHROPIC_API_KEY.' });
    68	    }
    69	    if (err instanceof Anthropic.RateLimitError) {
    70	      return res.status(429).json({ error: 'Rate limited by the Claude API. Wait a moment and retry.' });
    71	    }
    72	    if (err instanceof Anthropic.APIConnectionError) {
    73	      console.error('[chat] connection error:', err.message);
    74	      return res.status(502).json({ error: 'Could not reach the Claude API.' });
    75	    }
    76	    if (err instanceof Anthropic.APIError) {
    77	      console.error(`[chat] API error ${err.status}:`, err.message);
    78	      return res.status(502).json({ error: `Claude API error (${err.status}).` });
    79	    }
    80	    console.error('[chat] unexpected error:', err);
    81	    res.status(500).json({ error: 'Unexpected server error.' });
    82	  }
    83	});
    84	
    85	app.listen(PORT, () => console.log(`okr-viewer service on http://localhost:${PORT} (model: ${MODEL})`));
===== service/prompt.js =====
     1	import { RESPOND_INPUT_SCHEMA } from '@okr-viewer/schema';
     2	
     3	// The model ID lives here and only here.
     4	// Check https://docs.claude.com/en/docs/about-claude/models for the current
     5	// Sonnet-class ID before trusting this string.
     6	export const MODEL = 'claude-sonnet-5';
     7	
     8	// One tool, forced. Claude must call it exactly once, so we get structured
     9	// output in a single round trip with no tool_result continuation.
    10	//
    11	// The input schema is the shared chat-response schema with every $ref
    12	// flattened (Claude cannot follow external refs). That means the field
    13	// descriptions the model reads and the rules the service enforces are the
    14	// same text, generated from one source in schema/src/.
    15	export const RESPOND_TOOL = {
    16	  name: 'respond',
    17	  description: 'Reply to the user about their OKR tree. Always call this exactly once.',
    18	  input_schema: RESPOND_INPUT_SCHEMA
    19	};
    20	
    21	export function systemPrompt(tree, selectedNodeId) {
    22	  return `You are an OKR coach embedded in a 3D visualization of a company's objectives and key results. The user sees the tree as a graph and talks to you about it. You answer by calling the \`respond\` tool exactly once.
    23	
    24	## The tree
    25	Nodes are flat with parent pointers. Levels: company (the single root) → objective (team-level) → kr (key result). \`contributes\` (0–1) is a hand-assessed score of how well a node supports its parent; anything below 0.4 is a misalignment worth calling out. A KR with an empty or vague \`metric\` or \`target\` is not measurable. A KR that describes an activity ("run an offsite", "attend events") rather than an outcome is weak even if it has a number.
    26	
    27	<tree>
    28	${JSON.stringify(tree.nodes)}
    29	</tree>
    30	
    31	${
    32	  selectedNodeId
    33	    ? `The user currently has node "${selectedNodeId}" selected. Assume "this", "it", or "the selected one" refers to that node unless context says otherwise.`
    34	    : 'No node is currently selected.'
    35	}
    36	
    37	## How to respond
    38	- \`reply\`: 1–3 conversational sentences. Refer to nodes by their label, never by id. No JSON, no bullet lists.
    39	- \`highlight\`: the ids of every node your reply talks about. Be generous — the camera flies to these and they pulse, which is how the user follows along. Include the parent when the relationship matters. Only use ids that exist in the tree, or that you are adding in this same response.
    40	- \`actions\`: only when the user asks for a change (rewrite, fix, move, add, remove, make measurable, tighten, etc.). Send an empty array for questions.
    41	
    42	## Actions
    43	- { "op": "edit", "id", "fields": { label?, owner?, metric?, target?, contributes? } } — rewriting a KR to be measurable means a label that states the outcome, a concrete \`metric\`, and a \`target\` with a baseline and a goal (e.g. "31% → 50% by Q4"). Raise \`contributes\` when the fix actually makes it support its parent.
    44	- { "op": "relink", "id", "fields": { "parent" } } — move a node under a different parent. Follow it with an edit that updates \`contributes\` for the new fit.
    45	- { "op": "add", "id", "fields": { level, parent, label, owner, metric, target, contributes } } — new ids must be unique and of the form "kr-<short-slug>" or "obj-<short-slug>".
    46	- { "op": "delete", "id" } — removes the node and everything under it. Only when explicitly asked.
    47	- Never invent ids. Never change a node's level. Never touch the company objective unless asked.
    48	- When you edit, say what changed and why in plain language.
    49	
    50	Be direct and specific. A good OKR coach names the problem ("this is an activity, not an outcome") and proposes the fix.`;
    51	}
===== service/validate.js =====
     1	// Claude will occasionally invent node IDs. Everything it sends back is
     2	// filtered against the tree the browser sent us, so nothing unknown reaches
     3	// the renderer.
     4	//
     5	// Two layers, and the split matters:
     6	//
     7	//   @okr-viewer/schema's validateAction()  — shape. Is this an action at all?
     8	//   the checks below                       — reference. Does it fit *this* tree?
     9	//
    10	// Shape lives in the package because the browser needs the same answer. The
    11	// referential checks are tree-relative and stay here.
    12	
    13	import { validateAction } from '@okr-viewer/schema';
    14	
    15	// Keep only well-formed turns, cap the length, and merge consecutive
    16	// same-role turns so the API always sees strict user/assistant alternation.
    17	export function sanitizeHistory(history, maxTurns = 12) {
    18	  const turns = (Array.isArray(history) ? history : [])
    19	    .filter((m) => m && (m.role === 'user' || m.role === 'assistant') && typeof m.content === 'string' && m.content.trim())
    20	    .slice(-maxTurns)
    21	    .map((m) => ({ role: m.role, content: m.content.trim() }));
    22	  while (turns.length && turns[0].role !== 'user') turns.shift();
    23	  return mergeTurns(turns);
    24	}
    25	
    26	export function mergeTurns(turns) {
    27	  const out = [];
    28	  for (const t of turns) {
    29	    const last = out[out.length - 1];
    30	    if (last && last.role === t.role) last.content += '\n\n' + t.content;
    31	    else out.push({ ...t });
    32	  }
    33	  return out;
    34	}
    35	
    36	export function validateResponse(tree, input) {
    37	  const known = new Set(tree.nodes.map((n) => n.id));
    38	  const parentOf = new Map(tree.nodes.map((n) => [n.id, n.parent]));
    39	  const dropped = [];
    40	  const actions = [];
    41	
    42	  const isAncestorOrSelf = (candidate, id) => {
    43	    let cur = candidate;
    44	    for (let i = 0; cur != null && i < 1000; i++) {
    45	      if (cur === id) return true;
    46	      cur = parentOf.get(cur) ?? null;
    47	    }
    48	    return false;
    49	  };
    50	
    51	  const validParent = (id, parent) => parent != null && known.has(parent) && !isAncestorOrSelf(parent, id);
    52	
    53	  for (const a of Array.isArray(input?.actions) ? input.actions : []) {
    54	    const reject = (reason) => dropped.push({ action: a, reason });
    55	
    56	    // Shape first: op, id and the per-op field whitelist all come from the
    57	    // shared schema, so anything past here has the keys it claims to have.
    58	    const shape = validateAction(a);
    59	    if (!shape.ok) {
    60	      reject(shape.errors.join('; '));
    61	      continue;
    62	    }
    63	
    64	    const { op, id } = a;
    65	    const fields = { ...a.fields };
    66	
    67	    if (op === 'edit' || op === 'relink') {
    68	      if (!known.has(id)) { reject(`unknown id ${id}`); continue; }
    69	      if ('parent' in fields && !validParent(id, fields.parent)) {
    70	        reject(`bad parent ${fields.parent} for ${id}`);
    71	        delete fields.parent;
    72	      }
    73	      // A relink whose only field was the rejected parent has nothing left.
    74	      if (!Object.keys(fields).length) { reject('no usable fields'); continue; }
    75	      if ('parent' in fields) parentOf.set(id, fields.parent);
    76	      actions.push({ op, id, fields });
    77	    } else if (op === 'add') {
    78	      if (known.has(id)) { reject(`add with existing id ${id}`); continue; }
    79	      // The model is never allowed to mint a second company root.
    80	      if (!fields.level || fields.level === 'company') fields.level = 'kr';
    81	      if (!(fields.parent != null && known.has(fields.parent))) { reject(`add with bad parent ${fields.parent}`); continue; }
    82	      known.add(id);
    83	      parentOf.set(id, fields.parent);
    84	      actions.push({ op, id, fields });
    85	    } else if (op === 'delete') {
    86	      if (!known.has(id)) { reject(`unknown id ${id}`); continue; }
    87	      const gone = new Set([id]);
    88	      let grew = true;
    89	      while (grew) {
    90	        grew = false;
    91	        for (const [child, parent] of parentOf) {
    92	          if (gone.has(parent) && !gone.has(child)) { gone.add(child); grew = true; }
    93	        }
    94	      }
    95	      for (const g of gone) { known.delete(g); parentOf.delete(g); }
    96	      actions.push({ op, id });
    97	    }
    98	  }
    99	
   100	  const highlight = [
   101	    ...new Set((Array.isArray(input?.highlight) ? input.highlight : []).filter((id) => typeof id === 'string' && known.has(id)))
   102	  ];
   103	
   104	  const reply =
   105	    typeof input?.reply === 'string' && input.reply.trim()
   106	      ? input.reply.trim()
   107	      : actions.length
   108	        ? 'Done.'
   109	        : "I couldn't come up with an answer for that — try rephrasing.";
   110	
   111	  return { reply, actions, highlight, dropped };
   112	}
===== web/src/lib/tree.js =====

     1	// @okr-viewer/schema — the one authoritative description of the OKR document,
     2	// shared by web/ and service/.
     3	//
     4	// Two layers, kept apart on purpose (see the plan's Decision 6):
     5	//

     8	//                        exactly one root — things JSON Schema cannot express.

37	    36	/** Document format version this build reads and writes. */

39	    38	
40	    39	/**
41	    40	 * `PACKAGE_VERSION` — version of @okr-viewer/schema itself.
42	    41	 * `RESPOND_INPUT_SCHEMA` — the `respond` tool's input_schema, with every $ref flattened.
43	    42	 */

46	    45	/** `migrate(document, fromVersion)` and the MIGRATIONS ladder it walks. */
47	    46	export { MIGRATIONS, migrate };
48	    47	
49	    48	// ── rendering Ajv errors ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
50	    49	//
51	    50	// Ajv reports the root as '' and nested paths as '/tree/nodes/0/id'. Render
52	    51	// that as something a human can act on without leaking Ajv's shape.
53	    52	
54	    53	// Read a value out of `data` by Ajv instancePath ('' is the root).
55	    54	function at(data, instancePath) {
56	    55	  if (!instancePath) return data;
57	    56	  let cur = data;
58	    57	  for (const raw of instancePath.slice(1).split('/')) {
59	    58	    if (cur == null) return undefined;
60	    59	    cur = cur[raw.replace(/~1/g, '/').replace(/~0/g, '~')];
61	    60	  }
62	    61	  return cur;
63	    62	}
64	    63	
65	    64	const quote = (v) => JSON.stringify(v);
66	    65	
67	    66	// Turn Ajv's error objects into "path: message" strings, optionally shifted
68	    67	// under a prefix so a branch validated on its own still reports the path the
69	    68	// caller sees (`/actions/0/fields`, not `/fields`).
70	    69	function renderErrors(errors, prefix = '') {
71	    70	  if (!errors?.length) return ['failed validation'];
72	    71	
73	    72	  // Merge allowed values per path: one enum failure per line, not per branch.
74	    73	  const allowedByPath = new Map();
75	    74	  for (const e of errors) {
76	    75	    if (e.keyword !== 'enum' || !Array.isArray(e.params?.allowedValues)) continue;
77	    76	    const set = allowedByPath.get(e.instancePath) ?? new Set();
78	    77	    for (const v of e.params.allowedValues) set.add(v);
79	    78	    allowedByPath.set(e.instancePath, set);
80	    79	  }
81	    80	
82	    81	  const seen = new Set();
83	    82	  const out = [];
84	    83	  for (const e of errors) {
85	    84	    const path = prefix + e.instancePath || '/';
86	    85	    let text;
87	    86	    if (e.keyword === 'oneOf') continue; // the branch errors already say what is wrong
88	    87	    else if (e.keyword === 'enum') {
89	    88	      text = `${path}: must be one of ${[...allowedByPath.get(e.instancePath)].map(quote).join(', ')}`;
90	    89	    } else if (e.keyword === 'additionalProperties') {
91	    90	      text = `${path}: unknown property "${e.params.additionalProperty}"`;
92	    91	    } else {
93	    92	      text = `${path}: ${e.message ?? 'is invalid'}`;
94	    93	    }
95	    94	    if (seen.has(text)) continue;
96	    95	    seen.add(text);
97	    96	    out.push(text);
98	    97	  }
99	    98	  return out.length ? out : [`${prefix || '/'}: failed validation`];
100	    99	}
101	   100	
102	   101	// ── actions: oneOf, narrowed by hand ────────────────────────────────────────
103	   102	
104	   103	const ACTION_BRANCHES = {
105	   104	  edit: _validateEditAction,
106	   105	  relink: _validateRelinkAction,
107	   106	  add: _validateAddAction,
108	   107	  delete: _validateDeleteAction
109	   108	};
110	   109	const ACTION_OPS = Object.keys(ACTION_BRANCHES);
111	   110	
112	   111	// Ajv reports a failed oneOf as all four branches' complaints at once, and a
113	   112	// resolved $ref rewrites schemaPath relative to the branch — so there is no way
114	   113	// to tell from the errors which branch was meant. Read the `op` ourselves and
115	   114	// validate only that branch.
116	   115	function actionErrors(value, prefix) {
117	   116	  const root = prefix || '/';
118	   117	  if (!value || typeof value !== 'object' || Array.isArray(value)) return [`${root}: must be an object`];
119	   118	  if (!('op' in value)) return [`${root}: must have required property 'op'`];
120	   119	  const branch = ACTION_BRANCHES[value.op];
121	   120	  if (!branch) return [`${prefix}/op: must be one of ${ACTION_OPS.map(quote).join(', ')}`];
122	   121	  if (branch(value)) return []; // the branch is happy; the oneOf must have matched more than one
123	   122	  return renderErrors(branch.errors, prefix);
124	   123	}
125	   124	
126	   125	// Replace every oneOf failure over an Action with that branch's own errors.
127	   126	// Applies to a bare action and to actions nested in a chat response alike.
128	   127	function refineActionErrors(errors, data) {
129	   128	  const prefixes = errors.filter((e) => e.keyword === 'oneOf').map((e) => e.instancePath);
130	   129	  if (!prefixes.length) return renderErrors(errors);
131	   130	  const untouched = errors.filter((e) => !prefixes.some((p) => e.instancePath.startsWith(p)));
132	   131	  const refined = prefixes.flatMap((p) => actionErrors(at(data, p), p));
133	   132	  return [...(untouched.length ? renderErrors(untouched) : []), ...refined];
134	   133	}
135	   134	
136	   135	// ── structural validation ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
137	   136	
138	   137	// The precompiled validators park failures on `.errors`, which is overwritten
139	   138	// on the next call — so read it immediately.
140	   139	function wrap(validator, { actions = false } = {}) {
141	   140	  return (data) => {
142	   141	    if (validator(data)) return { ok: true, value: data };
143	   142	    const errors = validator.errors ?? [];
144	   143	    return { ok: false, errors: actions ? refineActionErrors(errors, data) : renderErrors(errors) };
145	   144	  };
146	   145	}
147	   146	
148	   147	/** @type {(data: unknown) => { ok: true, value: any } | { ok: false, errors: string[] }} */
149	   148	export const validateOkrDocument = wrap(_validateOkrDocument, { actions: true });
150	   149	export const validateOkrTree = wrap(_validateOkrTree);
151	   150	export const validateOkrNode = wrap(_validateOkrNode);
152	   151	export const validateCompany = wrap(_validateCompany);
153	   152	export const validateHistory = wrap(_validateHistory, { actions: true });
154	   153	export const validateAction = wrap(_validateAction, { actions: true });
155	   154	export const validateChatRequest = wrap(_validateChatRequest);
156	   155	export const validateChatResponse = wrap(_validateChatResponse, { actions: true });
157	   156	
158	   157	// ── semantic validation ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
159	   158	
160	   159	const LADDER = { company: 0, objective: 1, kr: 2 };
161	   160	
162	   161	/**
163	   162	 * Relational checks JSON Schema cannot express. Assumes the tree is already
164	   163	 * structurally valid; run a validate* first.
165	   164	 *
166	   165	 * Errors make the tree unusable. Warnings are style: the level ladder is only
167	   166	 * a warning because validateResponse and apply.js both allow free relinking,
168	   167	 * so a tree mid-edit can legitimately sit off-ladder.
169	   168	 */
170	   169	export function checkTreeSemantics(tree) {
171	   170	  const errors = [];
172	   171	  const warnings = [];
173	   172	  const nodes = tree?.nodes;
174	   173	  if (!Array.isArray(nodes)) return { errors: ['/nodes: must be an array'], warnings };
175	   174	
176	   175	  const byId = new Map();
177	   176	  for (const [i, n] of nodes.entries()) {
178	   177	    if (byId.has(n.id)) errors.push(`/nodes/${i}/id: duplicate node id "${n.id}"`);
179	   178	    else byId.set(n.id, n);
180	   179	  }
181	   180	
182	   181	  const roots = nodes.filter((n) => n.parent == null);
183	   182	  if (!roots.length) errors.push('/nodes: no root node (exactly one node must have parent: null)');
184	   183	  else if (roots.length > 1) {
185	   184	    errors.push(`/nodes: ${roots.length} root nodes (${roots.map((n) => n.id).join(', ')}), expected exactly one`);
186	   185	  }
187	   186	  for (const r of roots) if (r.level !== 'company') warnings.push(`/nodes: root "${r.id}" has level "${r.level}", expected "company"`);
188	   187	  for (const n of nodes) {
189	   188	    if (n.parent == null && n.level === 'company') continue;
190	   189	    if (n.level === 'company' && n.parent != null) warnings.push(`/nodes: "${n.id}" has level "company" but is not the root`);
191	   190	  }
192	   191	
193	   192	  for (const [i, n] of nodes.entries()) {
194	   193	    if (n.parent == null) continue;
195	   194	    const parent = byId.get(n.parent);
196	   195	    if (!parent) {
197	   196	      errors.push(`/nodes/${i}/parent: "${n.id}" points at missing parent "${n.parent}"`);
198	   197	      continue;
199	   198	    }
200	   199	    if (LADDER[n.level] !== LADDER[parent.level] + 1) {
201	   200	      warnings.push(`/nodes/${i}/level: "${n.id}" (${n.level}) hangs off "${parent.id}" (${parent.level})`);
202	   201	    }
203	   202	  }
204	   203	
205	   204	  // Walk up from every node; anything that does not terminate at a root is in
206	   205	  // or below a cycle. Report each cycle once, by its lowest-sorting member.
207	   206	  const reported = new Set();
208	   207	  for (const start of nodes) {
209	   208	    const seen = new Set();
210	   209	    let cur = start;
211	   210	    while (cur && cur.parent != null) {
212	   211	      if (seen.has(cur.id)) break;
213	   212	      seen.add(cur.id);
214	   213	      const next = byId.get(cur.parent);
215	   214	      if (!next) break; // already reported as a missing parent
216	   215	      if (seen.has(next.id)) {
217	   216	        const cycle = [...seen].filter((id) => byId.has(id)).sort();
218	   217	        const key = cycle.join(',');
219	   218	        if (!reported.has(key)) {
220	   219	          reported.add(key);
221	   220	          errors.push(`/nodes: parent cycle through ${cycle.join(' -> ')}`);
222	   221	        }
223	   222	        break;
224	   223	      }
225	   224	      cur = next;
226	   225	    }
227	   226	  }
228	   227	
229	   228	  return { errors, warnings };
230	   229	}
231	   230	
232	   231	// ── documents ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
233	   232	
234	   233	/**
235	   234	 * Stamp a tree (and optionally company/history) into an exportable document.
236	   235	 * @returns {object} an OkrDocument at the current SCHEMA_VERSION
237	   236	 */
238	   237	export function createDocument({ tree, company, history, meta } = {}) {
239	   238	  const doc = {
240	   239	    schemaVersion: SCHEMA_VERSION,
241	   240	    meta: {
242	   241	      ...meta,
243	   242	      exportedAt: meta?.exportedAt ?? new Date().toISOString(),
244	   243	      generator: meta?.generator ?? `@okr-viewer/schema ${PACKAGE_VERSION}`
245	   244	    },
246	   245	    tree
247	   246	  };
248	   247	  if (company !== undefined) doc.company = company;
249	   248	  if (history !== undefined) doc.history = history;
250	   249	  return doc;
251	   250	}
252	   251	
253	   252	/**
254	   253	 * Read a document written by createDocument: parse, version-check, migrate,
255	   254	 * validate structurally, then check semantics.
256	   255	 *
257	   256	 * @param {string | object} input JSON text or an already-parsed object
258	   257	 * @returns {{ ok: true, document: object, warnings: string[] }
259	   258	 *         | { ok: false, errors: string[], warnings: string[] }}
260	   259	 */
261	   260	export function parseDocument(input) {
262	   261	  let raw = input;
263	   262	  if (typeof input === 'string') {
264	   263	    try {
265	   264	      raw = JSON.parse(input);
266	   265	    } catch (err) {
267	   266	      return { ok: false, errors: [`/: not valid JSON (${err.message})`], warnings: [] };
268	   267	    }
269	   268	  }
270	   269	  if (!raw || typeof raw !== 'object' || Array.isArray(raw)) {
271	   270	    return { ok: false, errors: ['/: expected a JSON object'], warnings: [] };
272	   271	  }
273	   272	
274	   273	  const version = raw.schemaVersion;
275	   274	  if (!Number.isInteger(version) || version < 1) {
276	   275	    return { ok: false, errors: ['/schemaVersion: must be an integer >= 1'], warnings: [] };
277	   276	  }
278	   277	  if (version > SCHEMA_VERSION) {
279	   278	    return {
280	   279	      ok: false,
281	   280	      errors: [
282	   281	        `/schemaVersion: this file is version ${version}, but this build only reads up to ${SCHEMA_VERSION}. Update the app.`
283	   282	      ],
284	   283	      warnings: []
285	   284	    };
286	   285	  }
287	   286	
288	   287	  let document = raw;
289	   288	  if (version < SCHEMA_VERSION) {
290	   289	    const migrated = migrate(document, version);
291	   290	    if (!migrated.ok) return { ok: false, errors: migrated.errors, warnings: [] };
292	   291	    document = migrated.document;
293	   292	  }
294	   293	
295	   294	  const structural = validateOkrDocument(document);
296	   295	  if (!structural.ok) return { ok: false, errors: structural.errors, warnings: [] };
297	   296	
298	   297	  const { errors, warnings } = checkTreeSemantics(document.tree);
299	   298	  if (errors.length) return { ok: false, errors, warnings };
300	   299	  return { ok: true, document, warnings };
301	   300	}
302	===== schema/version.js =====
303	     1	// The document format version, in one place.
304	     2	//
305	     3	// Bump it when a change to schema/src/ breaks documents written by the previous
306	     4	// version, and add the matching step to migrations/index.js at the same time.
307	     5	// Additive changes (a new optional property) do not bump it.
308	     6	export const SCHEMA_VERSION = 1;

715	===== schema/scripts/build.mjs =====
716	     1	// Builds schema/dist/ from schema/src/. Six steps, each loud on failure:
717	     2	//
718	     3	//   1. meta-validate every src/*.schema.json against draft-07
719	     4	//   2. generate dist/types.d.ts with the json-schema-to-typescript fork
720	     5	//   3. precompile Ajv validators into dist/validators.js (+ .d.ts)
721	     6	//   4. copy the raw schemas into dist/json/
722	     7	//   5. flatten chat-response into dist/json/tool/respond-input.json for Claude
723	     8	//   6. tsc --noEmit over the generated types
724	     9	//
725	    10	// Run it as `npm run build:schema` from the repo root.
726	    11	
727	    12	import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
728	    13	import { createRequire } from 'node:module';
729	    14	import { mkdirSync, readdirSync, readFileSync, rmSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
730	    15	import { dirname, join } from 'node:path';
731	    16	import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
732	    17	
733	    18	import Ajv from 'ajv';
734	    19	import addFormats from 'ajv-formats';
735	    20	import standaloneCode from 'ajv/dist/standalone/index.js';
736	    21	import { compileFromFile } from 'json-schema-to-typescript';
737	    22	import $RefParser from '@apidevtools/json-schema-ref-parser';
738	    23	
739	    24	const HERE = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
740	    25	const PKG = join(HERE, '..');
741	    26	const SRC = join(PKG, 'src');
742	    27	const DIST = join(PKG, 'dist');
743	    28	
744	    29	const pkg = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(PKG, 'package.json'), 'utf8'));
745	    30	const step = (n, what) => console.log(`[schema] ${n}/6 ${what}`);
746	    31	const fail = (msg) => {
747	    32	  console.error(`[schema] BUILD FAILED: ${msg}`);
748	    33	  process.exit(1);
749	    34	};
750	    35	
751	    36	// The named validators consumers get, and the schema each one compiles from.
752	    37	// Keys must match the exports listed in index.js and dist/validators.d.ts.
753	    38	//
754	    39	// The four *Action entries compile a single oneOf branch each. Ajv reports a
755	    40	// oneOf failure as every branch's complaints at once, with no way to tell which
756	    41	// branch an error came from — resolving a $ref rewrites schemaPath relative to
757	    42	// the target. Validating the branch the `op` names gives a usable message.
758	    43	const VALIDATORS = {
759	    44	  validateOkrDocument: 'okr-document.schema.json',
760	    45	  validateOkrTree: 'okr-tree.schema.json',
761	    46	  validateOkrNode: 'okr-node.schema.json',
762	    47	  validateCompany: 'company.schema.json',
763	    48	  validateHistory: 'history.schema.json',
764	    49	  validateAction: 'action.schema.json',
765	    50	  validateChatRequest: 'chat-request.schema.json',
766	    51	  validateChatResponse: 'chat-response.schema.json',
767	    52	  validateEditAction: 'action.schema.json#/definitions/editAction',
768	    53	  validateRelinkAction: 'action.schema.json#/definitions/relinkAction',
769	    54	  validateAddAction: 'action.schema.json#/definitions/addAction',
770	    55	  validateDeleteAction: 'action.schema.json#/definitions/deleteAction'
771	    56	};
772	    57	
773	    58	const BASE = 'https://okr-viewer.dev/schema/v1/';
774	    59	
775	    60	rmSync(DIST, { recursive: true, force: true });
776	    61	mkdirSync(join(DIST, 'json', 'tool'), { recursive: true });
777	    62	
778	    63	const files = readdirSync(SRC).filter((f) => f.endsWith('.schema.json')).sort();
779	    64	if (!files.length) fail('no schemas found in src/');
780	    65	
781	    66	// ── 1. meta-validate ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
782	    67	step(1, `meta-validating ${files.length} schemas against draft-07`);
783	    68	
784	    69	// One Ajv instance for everything: adding all schemas up front lets the
785	    70	// cross-file $refs resolve by $id without a loader.
786	    71	const ajv = new Ajv({
787	    72	  allErrors: true,
788	    73	  strict: true,
789	    74	  allowUnionTypes: true, // our nullable ids are type: ['string', 'null']
790	    75	  code: { source: true, esm: true }
791	    76	});
792	    77	addFormats(ajv);
793	    78	
794	    79	const schemas = new Map();
795	    80	for (const f of files) {
796	    81	  const schema = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(SRC, f), 'utf8'));
797	    82	  if (schema.$id !== BASE + f) fail(`${f}: $id must be "${BASE + f}", got "${schema.$id}"`);
798	    83	  if (!ajv.validateSchema(schema)) {
799	    84	    fail(`${f} is not a valid draft-07 schema:\n${ajv.errorsText(ajv.errors, { separator: '\n' })}`);
800	    85	  }
801	    86	  schemas.set(f, schema);
802	    87	  ajv.addSchema(schema, schema.$id);
803	    88	}
804	    89	
805	    90	// ── 2. types ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
806	    91	step(2, 'generating dist/types.d.ts');
807	    92	
808	    93	const banner = `/* eslint-disable */
809	    94	/**
810	    95	 * Generated by json-schema-to-typescript (LudaThomas/json-schema-to-typescript#release)
811	    96	 * from ${pkg.name}@${pkg.version} schemas in schema/src/.
812	    97	 *
813	    98	 * DO NOT EDIT. Change the JSON Schema in schema/src/ and re-run \`npm run build:schema\`.
814	    99	 */`;
815	   100	
816	   101	const types = await compileFromFile(join(SRC, 'index.schema.json'), {
817	   102	  cwd: SRC + '/',
818	   103	  declareExternallyReferenced: true,
819	   104	  additionalProperties: false,
820	   105	  bannerComment: banner,
821	   106	  style: { singleQuote: true, semi: true, printWidth: 120 }
822	   107	});
823	   108	
824	   109	// The whole reason we use the fork: upstream inlines a $ref that has a
825	   110	// title/description sibling and suffixes the duplicate (OkrNode1). If that
826	   111	// regresses, the generated types silently fork into near-identical copies.
827	   112	const dupes = [...new Set(types.match(/^export (?:interface|type) (\w+?\d+)\b/gm) ?? [])];
828	   113	if (dupes.length) {
829	   114	  fail(
830	   115	    `dist/types.d.ts has suffixed duplicate types — the $ref-naming fix regressed:\n  ${dupes.join('\n  ')}\n` +
831	   116	      `Check that json-schema-to-typescript still resolves to the LudaThomas #release fork.`
832	   117	  );
833	   118	}
834	   119	
835	   120	writeFileSync(join(DIST, 'types.d.ts'), types);
836	   121	
837	   122	// ── 3. precompiled validators ───────────────────────────────────────────────
838	   123	step(3, `precompiling ${Object.keys(VALIDATORS).length} Ajv validators`);
839	   124	
840	   125	const byExport = {};
841	   126	for (const [name, target] of Object.entries(VALIDATORS)) {
842	   127	  const [file, pointer] = target.split('#');
843	   128	  if (!schemas.has(file)) fail(`validator ${name} points at missing schema ${file}`);
844	   129	  const id = BASE + target;
845	   130	  if (!ajv.getSchema(id)) fail(`validator ${name}: ${pointer ? `pointer #${pointer} ` : ''}does not resolve in ${file}`);
846	   131	  byExport[name] = id;
847	   132	}
848	   133	
849	   134	let validators;
850	   135	try {
851	   136	  validators = standaloneCode(ajv, byExport);
852	   137	} catch (err) {
853	   138	  fail(`Ajv standalone compilation failed: ${err.message}`);
854	   139	}
855	   140	
856	   141	// Ajv's esm output uses `export` for the validators but still reaches for its
857	   142	// own runtime helpers with `require()`, which throws in an ES module and would
858	   143	// not survive bundling either. Hoist each one into a real default import; for
859	   144	// these CJS modules that binding is module.exports, so the `.default` and
860	   145	// `.fullFormats` lookups after it still resolve.
861	   146	// The specifiers are deep paths into CJS packages ("ajv/dist/runtime/ucs2length"),
862	   147	// which require() resolves extensionlessly but ESM does not.
863	   148	const required = [...new Set([...validators.matchAll(/require\("([^"]+)"\)/g)].map((m) => m[1]))];
864	   149	const imports = required.map(
865	   150	  (mod, i) => `import __dep${i} from ${JSON.stringify(/\.[a-z]+$/.test(mod) ? mod : `${mod}.js`)};`
866	   151	);
867	   152	for (const [i, mod] of required.entries()) {
868	   153	  validators = validators.split(`require(${JSON.stringify(mod)})`).join(`__dep${i}`);
869	   154	}
870	   155	if (/\brequire\s*\(/.test(validators)) fail('validators.js still calls require() after the ESM rewrite');
871	   156	
872	   157	writeFileSync(
873	   158	  join(DIST, 'validators.js'),
874	   159	  `/* Generated by schema/scripts/build.mjs. DO NOT EDIT. */\n${imports.join('\n')}\n${validators}`
875	   160	);
876	   161	if (required.length) console.log(`[schema]     hoisted ${required.length} Ajv runtime require() calls to imports`);
877	   162	
878	   163	const validatorTypes = `/* Generated by schema/scripts/build.mjs. DO NOT EDIT. */
879	   164	/** An Ajv precompiled validator: returns true/false and parks failures on \`.errors\`. */
880	   165	export interface CompiledValidator {
881	   166	  (data: unknown): boolean;
882	   167	  errors?: null | { instancePath: string; schemaPath: string; keyword: string; message?: string; params: Record<string, unknown> }[];
883	   168	}
884	   169	${Object.keys(VALIDATORS)
885	   170	  .map((name) => `export declare const ${name}: CompiledValidator;`)
886	   171	  .join('\n')}
887	   172	`;
888	   173	writeFileSync(join(DIST, 'validators.d.ts'), validatorTypes);
889	   174	
890	   175	// ── 4. raw schema copies ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
891	   176	step(4, `copying ${files.length} schemas to dist/json/`);
892	   177	for (const [f, schema] of schemas) {
893	   178	  writeFileSync(join(DIST, 'json', f), JSON.stringify(schema, null, 2) + '\n');
894	   179	}
895	   180	
896	   181	// ── 5. flattened tool schema ────────────────────────────────────────────────
897	   182	step(5, 'flattening dist/json/tool/respond-input.json');
898	   183	
899	   184	// Claude's `input_schema` cannot follow external $refs, so the tool gets a
900	   185	// fully dereferenced copy. Descriptions carry over from src/, which is why
901	   186	// every property in the schemas is written for the model as well as for us.
902	   187	// Dereferencing splices whole files in, so the inlined copies bring their own
903	   188	// $schema/$id and their now-unreachable definitions with them. Strip those
904	   189	// everywhere, not just at the root.
905	   190	const DROP = new Set(['$schema', '$id', '$comment', 'definitions']);
906	   191	const prune = (node, seen = new Map()) => {
907	   192	  if (Array.isArray(node)) return node.map((v) => prune(v, seen));
908	   193	  if (!node || typeof node !== 'object') return node;
909	   194	  if (seen.has(node)) return seen.get(node);
910	   195	  const out = {};
911	   196	  seen.set(node, out);
912	   197	  for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(node)) if (!DROP.has(k)) out[k] = prune(v, seen);
913	   198	  return out;
914	   199	};
915	   200	
916	   201	const tool = prune(await $RefParser.dereference(join(SRC, 'chat-response.schema.json')));
917	   202	tool.required = ['reply', 'actions', 'highlight'];
918	   203	
919	   204	const leftover = JSON.stringify(tool).match(/"\$(ref|id|schema)"/g);
920	   205	if (leftover) fail(`respond-input.json still contains ${leftover.join(', ')} after dereferencing`);
921	   206	
922	   207	writeFileSync(join(DIST, 'json', 'tool', 'respond-input.json'), JSON.stringify(tool, null, 2) + '\n');
923	   208	
924	   209	// index.js is bundled for the browser as well as loaded by the service, so it
925	   210	// cannot read package.json off disk or use a JSON import attribute. Bake both
926	   211	// into a plain ESM module instead.
927	   212	writeFileSync(
928	   213	  join(DIST, 'meta.js'),
929	   214	  `/* Generated by schema/scripts/build.mjs. DO NOT EDIT. */
930	   215	export const PACKAGE_VERSION = ${JSON.stringify(pkg.version)};
931	   216	export const RESPOND_INPUT_SCHEMA = ${JSON.stringify(tool, null, 2)};
932	   217	`
933	   218	);
934	   219	writeFileSync(
935	   220	  join(DIST, 'meta.d.ts'),
936	   221	  `/* Generated by schema/scripts/build.mjs. DO NOT EDIT. */
937	   222	export declare const PACKAGE_VERSION: string;
938	   223	export declare const RESPOND_INPUT_SCHEMA: Record<string, unknown>;
939	   224	`
940	   225	);
941	   226	
942	   227	// ── 6. type-check ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
943	   228	step(6, 'type-checking generated declarations');
944	   229	
945	   230	// npm hoists devDeps to the workspace root, so resolve tsc rather than
946	   231	// assuming it sits in schema/node_modules.
947	   232	const tsc = createRequire(import.meta.url).resolve('typescript/bin/tsc');
948	   233	try {
949	   234	  // tsconfig.check.json rather than CLI flags: it pins lib/moduleResolution and
950	   235	  // sets `types: []`, so the check sees our declarations and nothing a hoisted
951	   236	  // @types/* package happens to drag in.
952	   237	  execFileSync(process.execPath, [tsc, '-p', 'tsconfig.check.json'], { cwd: PKG, stdio: 'pipe', encoding: 'utf8' });
953	   238	} catch (err) {
954	   239	  fail(`tsc rejected the generated declarations:\n${err.stdout ?? ''}${err.stderr ?? ''}`);
955	   240	}
956	   241	
957	   242	console.log(`[schema] built ${pkg.name}@${pkg.version} → dist/`);
958	===== schema/migrations/index.js =====
959	     1	// One function per major step of the document format.
960	     2	//
961	     3	// MIGRATIONS[n] upgrades a document written at schemaVersion n to n + 1. It
962	     4	// receives a document it may mutate freely (parseDocument hands it a value the
963	     5	// caller has already given up) and returns the upgraded document.
964	     6	//
965	     7	// v1 is the first format, so the ladder is empty. When SCHEMA_VERSION becomes
966	     8	// 2, add MIGRATIONS[1] here and a round-trip fixture under test/fixtures/.
967	     9	
968	    10	import { SCHEMA_VERSION } from '../version.js';
969	    11	
970	    12	/** @type {Record<number, (document: any) => any>} */
971	    13	export const MIGRATIONS = {};
972	    14	
973	    15	/**
974	    16	 * Walk a document up the ladder from `fromVersion` to the current version.
975	    17	 *
976	    18	 * @param {object} document
977	    19	 * @param {number} fromVersion the document's own schemaVersion
978	    20	 * @returns {{ ok: true, document: object } | { ok: false, errors: string[] }}
979	    21	 */
980	    22	export function migrate(document, fromVersion) {
981	    23	  let current = document;
982	    24	  for (let v = fromVersion; v < SCHEMA_VERSION; v++) {
983	    25	    const step = MIGRATIONS[v];
984	    26	    if (!step) {
985	    27	      return {
986	    28	        ok: false,
987	    29	        errors: [`/schemaVersion: no migration from version ${v} to ${v + 1}; this file is too old to read.`]
988	    30	      };
989	    31	    }
990	    32	    try {
991	    33	      current = step(current);
992	    34	    } catch (err) {
993	    35	      return { ok: false, errors: [`/schemaVersion: migration ${v} -> ${v + 1} failed (${err.message})`] };
994	    36	    }
995	    37	    current.schemaVersion = v + 1;
996	    38	  }
997	    39	  return { ok: true, document: current };
998	    40	}
999
